A non-pooled migrate only moved the failure to the first pooled open (11 failures in 803 cycles); a bounded retry of the initialisation gave 0 test failures in 798 cycles (12 retries, all recovered). The same race also hits the drop's open of the `master` pool (3 events). Package `Alveara_TestSpeed_Parallelism_ModeB_Investigation_v1.zip`.
  - Notes: the hammer is an accelerator (about 1,500 times the natural tick rate), natural rate is about 1 in 4,000 to 5,000 fixtures. Proposal (bounded, logged retry of the pool-shutdown exception at fixture initialisation and at the drop) awaits independent review; nothing implemented. Not pushed.

- [x] TESTQ-BACKUPCRYPTO-TAMPER: the BackupCrypto tamper flake fixed (test and one production catch), the vitest margins widened, the 17 test-project build warnings cleared
  - Date: 2026-10-11
  - Session: CC-20261008-k4q9
  - What changed: (1) `BackupCryptoTests.Altering_any_part_of_the_file_is_detected_and_never_yields_plaintext` flipped a bit at fixed offsets, and offset 400 landed (about 1 file in 70, when the RSA ciphertext lost a leading zero byte) on the encrypted-data packet's version byte, which OpenPGP's integrity check does not cover and BouncyCastle does not validate; flipping it always decrypts to the identical plaintext, so the old assertion was wrong, not the product. The test now takes every position from the file's own structure, and two new tests pin the version-byte property (found by construction) and a regression for (2). (2) A real defect found while proving the test deterministic: damaging the first byte of the encrypted data made BouncyCastle throw `UnsupportedPacketVersionException` in about 11% of files, which escaped `BackupCrypto.DecryptAsync` as an unclassified exception instead of `corrupt_or_tampered`; it is now in the catch list (`src/Alveara.Api/Architecture/Backup/BackupCrypto.cs`). (3) `vitest.config.ts` `testTimeout` 5 s to 15 s and `src/test/setup.ts` Testing Library `asyncUtilTimeout` 1 s to 5 s (widening only). (4) 17 warnings (xUnit2013, xUnit2000, xUnit2031, CS8619, CS8620, CS8631, EF1002) fixed in 15 test files with no assertion changed.
  - Verification: full serial backend 2729 of 2729 (43 m 16 s; the 2 new tests are the only addition); the new regression test failed before the production fix with the raw exception; a temporary 150-file loop (alternating 5 kB and 2 MB files, every position) ran with 0 failures against the final code, and the old fixed-offset logic had failed 12 to 18 of 150 in the same loop with the earlier code; build 0 warnings and 0 errors; the 15 edited test classes 416 of 416; vitest 1325 of 1325 normally and in all three of three concurrent copies (the same stress had given 3 failures with only the timeout raised, which led to the second margin); mocked Playwright 136 of 136; repository checks 8 of 8; tsc, lint, build exit 0; leak counts unchanged (54 / 59 / 273 / 248). The real-backend walkthrough sweep was not rerun: nothing it exercises changed.
  - Notes: closes the disclosures about the BackupCrypto intermittent (STORY-014 and STORY-007 commit bodies) and the periodontal and BackupRecovery vitest timeouts (ALV-015-C01, STORY-007). Not pushed.

- [x] ALV-010-C01 step 3 of the build: storage integrity (check, damaged-file refusal, unreferenced-file listing)
  - Date: 2026-10-20
  - Session: CC-20261008-k4q9
  - What changed: `IBlobStorage` gains `GetInfoAsync` and `ListAsync` (a file's size and last write, and every stored file; temporary and stray files are not blobs). New `DocumentIntegrityService`: a stored file is Ok (present with the size and SHA-256 recorded at import), Missing or Corrupt; one check or a run over every document, each saved with its log entry in one transaction (`DocumentIntegrityChecked`, and `DocumentStorageChecked` with counts only, never a file name or patient); a file that cannot be read just now is skipped and counted, never marked corrupt; the unreferenced files older than an hour are listed (nothing is removed yet). The result is its own row per document (`DocumentIntegrity`), not columns on the document, so a check never moves a document's row version and never makes an open edit look stale: the step 1 migration (not yet pushed) was amended in place to do this (46 schema tests still green, new tests added). Opening refuses a missing file (`file_missing`, 404), a file whose size differs or that the last check found corrupt (`file_corrupt`, 409) and logs no open. New permission `ManageDocumentStorage` (Admin, Office manager) and `DocumentStorageController`: `GET /api/document-storage`, `POST /api/document-storage/check`, `POST /api/document-storage/documents/{id}/check`. `SchedulingApiHarness` can now give the API its own storage folder.
  - Verification: written test-first: `DocumentIntegrityServiceTests` 16 (red against a stub, then green), `DocumentStorageApiTests` 11 (red until the service was registered, then green), the permission matrix case and the schema tests for the new table (99 schema tests); mutation check, eleven breakages (the hash never compared, a missing file counted as ok, an unreadable file marked corrupt, no grace period, documents' own files listed as unreferenced, a run not logged, a corrupt file still served, a cut-short file still served, viewers allowed to run the check, plus two re-runs) each made 1 to 7 tests fail: two survivors exposed a test using files too new to matter and a mutant that did not compile; the test was strengthened and the mutants redone and caught; broader regression of the permission, storage, backup and document suites 663 of 663.
  - Notes: a run hashes every file, so it is for on-request or off-hours use (recorded in the class comment). Not pushed.

- [x] ALV-010-C01 step 4 of the build: review and removal of unreferenced stored files, and recovery from an interrupted import
  - Date: 2026-10-20
  - Session: CC-20261008-k4q9
  - What changed: `DocumentIntegrityService.RemoveOrphansAsync` and `POST /api/document-storage/orphans/remove` (`ManageDocumentStorage` plus CSRF; 1 to 500 file ids a reviewer names): each file gets its own outcome (Removed, AlreadyGone, StillReferenced, TooNew, Failed) after being re-checked at the moment it is removed (no document points to it; older than an hour); each removal is its own transaction that takes an application lock on the file (so simultaneous removals act once), saves a `DocumentOrphanRemoved` entry (the file id, never a name or a patient) and only then deletes the file, so a failed log write or a failed delete leaves the file and logs nothing; one failure never stops the others; nothing is ever removed automatically. Both ways an import can be interrupted now have a tested recovery: a crash after the file was stored but before the record was saved leaves a file the review lists after an hour and removes, a failed clean-up leaves the same (the importer was told nothing was imported), and a record whose file is lost later is found by the storage check and refused when opened. The test store can hold simultaneous callers at a gate so overlap is real, not left to timing.
  - Verification: written test-first: `DocumentOrphanServiceTests` 14 (13 red against a stub, then green) and 11 more cases in `DocumentStorageApiTests` (22 in all); 96 of 96 across the integrity, orphan, storage-route and import suites; mutation check, eight breakages (a referenced file removable, a file under an hour removable, the file deleted without its log entry saved, a failed removal reported as removed, one file named twice handled twice, 501 files accepted, no lock on simultaneous removals, viewers allowed to remove) each made 1 to 10 tests fail; the first run of the lock mutant survived because a delay did not make the callers overlap, so the test store gained a gate that holds the first callers until all have arrived, and the mutant is now caught and the test passes with the lock on; the simultaneous-removal test was run six more times with the lock on, all green.
  - Notes: if the entry's transaction cannot commit after the file was deleted (rare) the file is gone without an entry; the failure is logged by class and the outcome is Failed. Not pushed.

- [x] ALV-010-C01 step 5 of the build: library filters, thumbnails and the import safety matrix
  - Date: 2026-10-20
  - Session: CC-20261008-k4q9
  - What changed: `DocumentLibraryFilter` and `DocumentLibraryService.ListAsync(patientId, filter)`: a patient's library narrowed by category, encounter, tooth or region, date range (both ends included), status (Active by default, Inactive, All) and a word of the file name or description (matched literally and case-insensitively, `%` and `_` are not wildcards); the filters combine, a wrong value is refused naming its field (`metadata_invalid`: `toothKey`, `regionKey`, `from`, `text`, `status`, and on the route `category`, `encounter`, `to`) instead of being ignored; signed forms are listed unless a filter narrows to a document's own properties (the date range and the text apply to them by signing date and form title); the earlier `includeInactive` listing still works. `GET /api/patients/{id}/documents` takes these as query parameters. `GET /api/documents/{id}/thumbnail` serves a PNG or JPEG up to 5 MB as stored (the browser scales it), inline under the same safe headers, with the same missing and damaged-file checks as opening and no history row or log entry; other types are `not_an_image` (415) and larger pictures `thumbnail_too_large` (413). Preview is the existing content route (a deliberate open, logged), so no separate route. The stored-file checks are shared by opening and the thumbnail. Characterization tests pin the import protections: 61 cases of file names (folder parts dropped, device names, control and invisible characters, Windows-invalid characters, trailing dots and stream suffixes, 255 and 256 characters, blocked types dressed several ways) with the stored file always a generated id inside the storage folder and nothing created elsewhere, and of content against name (a program, a web page or a picture of another type under a harmless name).
  - Verification: written test-first: `DocumentLibraryFilterTests` 24 (all red against a stub, then green), 14 more cases in `DocumentLibraryApiTests` and `DocumentPathSafetyTests` 61 (these passed on first run, confirming STORY-010's rules already hold, so they are regression guards, proven by mutation); 61 of 61 across the library and original API tests; mutation check, twelve breakages (the folder part of a name kept, device names allowed, blocked types allowed, programs not recognized, a claimed type not compared, text wildcards unescaped, signed forms listed under a category filter, the start of the date range excluded, inactive documents shown by default, any type given a thumbnail, a wrong tooth in the filter ignored, a thumbnail without the sandbox policy) each made 1 to 11 tests fail.
  - Notes: no new dependency; thumbnails are not resized on the server (desktop or server-made thumbnails come later, behind the same route). A library is returned whole (no paging); realistic per-patient sizes are small. Not pushed.

- [x] ALV-009-C01 slice 4: the follow-up reminders on the durable job queue (ALV-N002)
  - Date: 2026-10-24
  - Session: CC-20261008-k4q9
  - What changed: `FollowUpReminderService.EnqueueDueAsync` now only ENQUEUES one job per task, due time and channel (key `followup-reminder:{task}:{due ticks}:{channel}`; pages of 200 so a big backlog cannot starve newer reminders; nobody to tell makes no job; a failed job for a reminder still owed gets a fresh set of attempts after a 5-minute cool-down), and the new `FollowUpReminderJobHandler` does the telling inside the ALV-N002 runner's transaction (notification, delivery record, `Reminded` event and audit entry commit with the job's receipt and status). The in-app channel stages into the runner's context through the new `IStagingReminderChannel`. `FollowUpReminderService.ProcessOnceAsync` is kept as the inline pass (enqueue, then run just the reminder jobs) so the STORY-009 reminder tests are unchanged; `BackgroundJobRunner` gained `ProcessOnceAsync(jobTypes)` so an inline run never claims jobs it has no handler for. The background scan loop only enqueues. A task with no owner is reminded to the office managers with the outcome `Unassigned`.
  - Verification: the 17 existing STORY-009 reminder tests pass unchanged through the job path (`FollowUpReminderTests` 16, `FollowUpReminderApiTests` 1); `FollowUpReminderJobTests` 13 passed (one job per task, due time and channel and nothing delivered by the scan; delivery once inside the runner's transaction; a job left claimed by a dead server recovered; a job whose effect had committed finished without a second reminder; the same job forced to run again changes nothing; failing all attempts under an audit outage then a fresh set of attempts after the cool-down and delivery once the audit works; no reset once the task is closed; a completed or re-dated task gets nothing; unassigned to the office managers; no job while nobody can be told; the inline pass leaves other jobs pending; the handlers and the in-app channel registered with no duplicate job type; a backlog of 230 worked through in two passes); with the follow-up, job, backup, work-queue, notification and architecture suites: 463 passed.
  - Notes: a reminder job is now capped at 3 attempts per set (the runner's rule), then waits for the cool-down: a reminder is still never given up on while it is owed. The reminder reaches the person within the scan interval (60 s, 3 s in walkthroughs) plus the job runner's 10 s poll. The negative control "reminder dedupe filter removed" (C1) is now an equivalent mutant: the existing job key makes a repeat harmless. Not pushed.

- [x] STORY-017 step 3: the merge schema
  - Date: 2026-10-25
  - Session: CC-20261008-k4q9
  - What changed: migration `AddPatientMerges`. New tables `PatientMerges` (the lineage: source, survivor, who, when, why, a merge key that makes a repeat the same merge; a patient is retired once), `PatientMergeMoves` (rows moved per table and column, zero too) and `PatientMergeResolutions` (a field kept or taken, with the survivor's replaced value kept; a record left with the retired patient), all append-only (51230); `Patients.MergedAtUtc` (a merged record stays in place, inactive, never reactivated or edited; marked only by a lineage with the same time, 51232-51233; a merged-away patient is never a survivor, 51231). The narrow exception: inside a transaction that sets the session value `alveara.patient_merge` to 1, the 51 immutable-record triggers on the 54 patient tables stand aside for an UPDATE, and a new `TR_<table>_MergeScope` trigger on each of the 54 refuses that UPDATE unless it sets nothing but the patient-id columns, and refuses any insert or delete (51234). The migration patches the existing triggers by script and its Down removes the patch exactly; Down refuses while any merge exists (51239). Entities in `Architecture/Patients/Merge/PatientMergeEntities.cs`, model in `Data/PatientMergeModel.cs`, `PatientOwnedData.MergeFlag/MergeScopeTrigger/Bookkeeping`.
  - Verification: `PatientMergeSchemaTests` 12 passed against a real SQL Server (lineage constraints and every refusal, append-only on all three tables, the merged mark and its time, survivor rule and chains, a patient id moved inside a merge for a plan, a document and a final completed referral while refused without the value and with the value 0, nothing but the patient id may be set, no insert or delete, a zero-row update is quiet, the value does not outlive its connection, a merged patient's dependants' guarantor can be repointed, the catalog shows a merge-scope trigger on every table listing every other column and every update trigger patched, rollback and roll-forward and refusal over a merge); with the registry, inventory (count 19), the earlier rollback tests that walk back through this migration, patient, architecture and backup suites: 405 related tests, 1 needed a fix (`NotificationSchemaTests` counted trigger names by prefix and now excludes the merge-scope triggers) and passed after; 10 negative controls on the migration SQL, all killed (2 survived at first as dead conditions: the survivor and rollback guards each had a second check that the merged-mark rule makes impossible; removed, not left unprovable).
  - Notes: finding for the owner: the immutable-record exception covers 51 triggers on the patient tables, not the ~27 tables first estimated; it includes the "final record never changes" guards (completed lab and referral cases, completed plan items, closed perio sessions), so a merge can move final records too. The link triggers (a child's patient id must equal its parent's) also stand aside during a merge, so the merge service in step 4 must re-check that every moved child still agrees with its parent before the transaction commits. Collision answers a merge can give are only these: keep the survivor's value, take the source's value, or leave the source's record with the retired patient (rows are never deleted). Not pushed.
- [x] STORY-017 step 4: the merge service
  - Date: 2026-10-25
  - Session: CC-20261008-k4q9
  - What changed: `Architecture/Patients/Merge/PatientMergeService.cs` (+ `PatientMergePlanner`, `PatientMergeCatalog`, `PatientMergeFields`, `PatientMergeViews`). `PreviewAsync` reads and changes nothing: field conflicts, fields the survivor has empty, rows to move per column, records that must stay, blockers. `MergeAsync` does the merge in ONE transaction: locks both patients, checks the versions the administrator reviewed, sets the merge session value, moves every registered patient-id column (the registry and the model decide the list), clears the value, checks that nothing is left on the retired patient and that every moved row still agrees with the row it points at, applies the answer to each field conflict (the survivor's replaced value is kept in the lineage), carries over an empty field and an absent household, writes the lineage, moves and resolutions, history rows and two audit entries (user and time), and marks the retired patient merged (inactive, in place, never deleted). A record the survivor already has its one of (read from the database's own unique indexes, filters included) stays with the retired patient together with everything that depends on it; if a staying record points at one that would move, the merge is blocked (`merge_blocked`, 409) and nothing changes. Repeat of the same Idempotency-Key is answered with the merge already made; the same key for another pair is `key_reused`. Also: migration `AddPatientMerges` reworked (still unpushed): the 61 immutable-record triggers that are INSTEAD OF now do the patient-id change themselves inside a merge (the first version made the update a silent no-op), a zero-row update passes, and the patch is removed by markers on rollback; `AuditEventTypes.PatientMerged`.
  - Verification: `PatientMergeServiceTests` 14 passed against a real SQL Server and a real chart (every registered column conserved: retired 0, survivor = both sums; lineage, history, two audit entries with user and time; preview changes nothing; field answers and what they replaced; collision stays with the retired record with its versions; blocked merge changes nothing; repeat and key reuse; merged records cannot be merged again; versions and reason required, stale version refused; three simultaneous requests make one merge; a failing audit write rolls everything back and the same request then succeeds; guarantors; household; the post-move check refuses a disagreeing row and a row left behind); 892 related tests passed (merge, registry, inventory, earlier rollback tests through the new Down, patient, architecture, backup/restore, audit, safety, treatment plan); 20 negative controls on the service all killed, plus the 11 on the migration from step 3.
  - Notes: findings: (1) rows-affected from an UPDATE is unreliable through INSTEAD OF triggers, so the move counts in the lineage come from a count taken before and are proved by the conservation test; (2) 61 INSTEAD OF triggers needed the bypass to perform the change, found only by running a real merge. Limits, stated plainly: identity fields (name, date of birth) are not reconcilable because they make the unique identity key, the survivor keeps its own; a collision on a record with dependants that point at something that must move blocks the merge instead of being split (a way to resolve those is for ALV-017-C01); who may merge is decided at the API in step 5. Not pushed.

- [x] ALV-017-C01 slice 5a: a merge keeps a ledger of the rows it moved, so it can be undone
  - Date: 2026-10-25
  - Session: CC-20261008-k4q9
  - What changed: migration `AddPatientUnmerge`: `PatientMergeMovedRows` (the ledger: merge, table, column and the row's key as it is after the move, so a table keyed by the patient id, a recall plan, is found again; append-only, 51236), `PatientUnmerges` (who, when, why, rows moved back; one per merge, one per key; append-only 51237; recorded only for a merge whose record is still merged, 51238), `PatientMerges.HasLedger`; the unique rule "a record is retired once" becomes a trigger so a record can be merged away again only after its earlier merge was undone (51244; a record whose merge was undone can then also be a survivor, 51231 adjusted); `TR_Patients_Merged` lets a merged record become active again only with a second session value (`alveara.patient_unmerge`), only through an undone merge, and only its active flag, merged mark and who and when change (the other columns are listed from the table's own columns when the migration runs); Down refuses over a ledger or an undone merge (51245). `PatientMergeService`: the lineage is saved first, each column's moved rows are written to the ledger just before they move, and the merge sets `HasLedger`.
  - Verification: `PatientUnmergeSchemaTests` 6 passed against a real SQL Server (the ledger holds exactly the rows that moved, per table and column, each key a row the survivor now owns, the recall plan under its new key, append-only, names refused; an undo is recorded once for a merge that still holds and never changed or deleted; an undo of a merge whose record is not merged is refused; one merge away at a time and again only after an undo, the survivor rule likewise; a merged record comes back only through an undone merge with the value and only the allowed columns, the check constraint answering first where it should; the migration back and forward and refused over a ledger); `PatientMergeSchemaTests` and the other merge classes, the guard, the review queue, the registry and inventory (22 classes), and the earlier rollback tests pass: 170 related tests, three assertions updated for the intended changes (a second merge of a record is now 51244 not a unique-index error; one more merge table).
  - Notes: merges made before this slice have no ledger (`HasLedger` false) and cannot be undone. The undo service, API and screen follow. Not pushed.
- [x] ALV-017-C01 slice 5b: undoing a merge
  - Date: 2026-10-25
  - Session: CC-20261008-k4q9
  - What changed: `PatientUnmergeService` (`Architecture/Patients/Merge/`): `PreviewAsync` and `UnmergeAsync`. It moves back exactly the rows the merge's ledger names, then checks every record still agrees with the one it points at; field values the merge took from the retired record are put back only while the surviving record still holds exactly that value (an edit made since is kept and said so; an empty field the merge filled is emptied again; a household the merge carried over is cleared if unchanged); it records the undo (who, when, why, rows moved back), makes the retired record active again through the narrow session value, writes history and two audit entries (user and time), all in one transaction; the merge's own lineage is untouched. It refuses, changing nothing: no confirmation or reason or key, an unknown merge (404), a merge already undone, a merge made before ledgers existed (`unmerge_unavailable`), a merge that no longer holds, a surviving record merged away since (`survivor_merged`: undo that first), a stale record, and records made after the merge that depend on rows that would move back (`unmerge_blocked`, naming the table). The preview does the same work and rolls it back, so what it promises is what an undo does. `HistoryAsync` and the merge log understand undone merges (`UndoneAtUtc`, `UndoneByName`, `CanUndo`); `PatientMergeFields` can clear an optional field.
  - Verification: `PatientUnmergeServiceTests` 12 passed against a real SQL Server and the real chart of the cross-domain test: a merge and its undo leave every registered column of both patients exactly as before (retired and surviving), nothing disagrees, the survivor's own phone is back and the filled email and household are gone, the account balance and entry count are the same, the retired record is active and unmarked, the original lineage stays, the undo has its own record, two audit entries with user and time and no phone number, history shows "Activated", the lineage page shows nothing current, the log shows the undo; the record can be merged away again after an undo and that merge undone in turn (two lineages, two undos); a field edited after the merge is kept; the preview changes nothing and promises exactly what the undo does; refusals as listed, nothing changed; a repeat with the same key is the undo already made; a merge without a ledger and one whose survivor was merged away are refused; a record linking a retired-chart row to a survivor-chart row after the merge stops the undo with the moves rolled back; a fault while the rows move back, when the undo is recorded, when the record is made active and when the audit is written each leaves everything as before, nothing leaks onto a pooled connection, and the same request then succeeds; 121 related tests passed.
  - Notes: not restored by design: a guarantor link from the surviving record to the retired one, which the merge cleared, and fields edited since. API and screen follow. Not pushed.
